\kappa>\frac{8(\log\lVert df\rVert/r)-2}{1-\alpha}.

We then consider another arbitrary parameter
\delta>0
which satisfies:

(7.3)

\delta<\min\Big(\frac{1}{8h(1-\alpha)},\frac{\alpha}{10(1-\alpha)},1/2\Big).

Choose also
\delta
small enough such that it satisfies also:

(7.4)

h+\kappa\delta<\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\big(\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})-2\lambda(f)/r\big).

Up to taking an iterate large enough, which will not change the rest of the proof since our analysis relies on entropy, we can suppose that:

(7.5)

\lambda(f)(1-\delta)\leq\log\lVert df\rVert\leq\lambda(f)(1+\delta).

Then, we select the parameter
\epsilon>0
, which will be the scale at which we are going to compute the entropy. Recall that by (
6.4
) and (
6.6
), we can take
\epsilon>0
such that for any
f
-invariant measure
m
:

(7.6)

h(f,m,\epsilon)\geq h(f,m)-\frac{\lambda(f)}{r}(1+\delta).

We also require that
\epsilon<\epsilon_{\Upsilon}(r)
, where
\epsilon_{\Upsilon}(r)
is the scale given by Yomdin Theorem
6.6
.

We also fix the integer parameter
N_{\star}
, which will be the frequency at which we will reparametrize. We choose it such that:

(7.7)

N_{\star}>\frac{10\log\Upsilon}{\alpha h}

where
\Upsilon:=\Upsilon(r)
is the constant from Yomdin Theorem
6.6
.

Step U.I. 2.

The
\nu_{1}
-rectangles.

Take any
K>8
. Define the map
H(t)=-t\log t-(1-t)\log(1-t)
. Choose also a parameter
\eta\in(0,1)
small such that:

(7.8)

\eta<\min\Big(\frac{\delta r}{32N_{\star}\log\lVert df\rVert},\frac{\delta}{40\log\big(24(K+1)+2\big)},\frac{\delta}{56\log\lVert 2df\rVert},\frac{\delta}{16h(1+\delta^{2})}\Big)\ \text{and}\ H(5\eta)<\frac{\delta}{16}.

We apply Proposition
3.11
to the measure
\nu_{1}
with these choices of
K
and
\eta
. It gives us a Pesin block
\hat{\Lambda}
, an integer
N\geq 1
, a foliation
\mathscr{F}^{u}
of an open set
V\supset\Lambda:=\pi(\hat{\Lambda})
and two families of
us
-rectangles
\mathcal{R}=(R_{1},\dots,R_{L})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{R}}=(\tilde{R}_{1},\dots,\tilde{R}_{L})
such that Properties
(MR1)
to
(MR5)
hold for some parameters
\alpha^{\prime},\sigma>0
sufficiently small. Choose
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\sigma
such that all the results from Sections
3
and
4
hold.

Up to reducing
\epsilon
, we also choose
\sigma
such that:

(7.9)

\sigma(1+K)<\epsilon<2\sigma(1+K).

Since
\epsilon
and
\sigma
are the last parameters we have chosen, we can reduce them as much as we want. We will do it in what follows, often to work with rectangles small enough compared to the size of the Pesin charts, which can be chosen uniformly since we work with charts of the Pesin block
\hat{\Lambda}
.

Let
\hat{U}_{1}
be the neighborhood of
\hat{\Lambda}
constructed in Lemma
3.5
and used in Property
(MR5)
for the integer
N
. Recall that we have
\pi(\hat{U}_{1})\subset\cup_{i}R_{i}
. Recall also that it satisfies:

\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{U}_{1})>1-\eta.

Since the most important property of Proposition
3.11
, Property
(MR5)
, holds for the iterate
f^{N}
, we will, from now on, fix
g=f^{N}
.

Step U.I. 3.

The small entropy part.

Let us deal with the small entropy part of the measure
\hat{\nu}
, namely the measure
\hat{\nu}_{0}
. We are going to show that we can find open sets with large
\hat{\nu}_{0}
-measure which admit covers by Bowen balls of small cardinality.

Claim 7.3
.

There exists an integer
N_{0}
with the following property. For any
n\geq N_{0}
, there exists an open set
\hat{U}_{0}:=\hat{U}_{0}(n)
and a finite set
\mathcal{C}_{0}:=\mathcal{C}_{0}(n)
such that:

–

\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{0})>1-\eta
and
\hat{\nu}_{0}(\partial\hat{U}_{0})=0
.

–

\hat{U}_{0}\subset\bigcup_{\hat{x}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}B_{g}\big(\hat{x},n,\epsilon\big)
.

–

|\mathcal{C}_{0}|\leq\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})n+o(n)\big)
.

Proof.

Since the measure
\hat{\nu}_{0}
may not be ergodic, let us consider an ergodic decomposition:

\hat{\nu}_{0}=\int_{M_{f}}\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{x}).

By our hypothesis,
\hat{\nu}_{0}
-almost every
\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}
satisfies
h(\hat{f},\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}})\leq h
, which implies that
h(\hat{g},\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}})\leq hN
. Since the
\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}
are ergodic, using (
6.2
), for
\hat{\nu}_{0}
-almost every
\hat{x}
we have:

\limsup_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{n}\inf_{\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}(\hat{A})>(1-\eta)^{1/2}}\log r_{\hat{g}}\big(n,\epsilon,\hat{A}\big)\leq Nh.

This implies the existence of an integer
n(\hat{x})\geq 1
such that for any
n\geq n(\hat{x})
, there exists a measurable set
\hat{A}_{n}(\hat{x})
, satisfying
\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}(\hat{A}_{n}(\hat{x}))>(1-\eta)^{1/2}
and:

r_{\hat{g}}\big(n,\epsilon,\hat{A}_{n}(\hat{x})\big)\leq\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2}/10)n\big).

Let
\mathcal{C}_{n}(\hat{x})\subset M_{f}
be a set of points such that
\#\mathcal{C}_{n}(\hat{x})\leq\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2}/10)n\big)
and:

\hat{A}_{n}(\hat{x})\subset\bigcup_{\hat{y}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}(\hat{x})}B_{\hat{g}}\big(\hat{y},n,\epsilon\big):=\hat{A}^{\prime}_{n}(\hat{x}).

Take now a compact set
\hat{K}
and a constant
r>0
such that
\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{K})>(1-\eta)^{1/2}
and the following properties hold.

–

There exists an integer
N_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{K}
and any
n\geq N_{0}
we have:

r_{\hat{g}}\big(n,\epsilon,\hat{A}^{\prime}_{n}(\hat{x})\big)\leq\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2}/10)n\big).

–

For two points
\hat{x},\hat{y}\in\hat{K}
such that
d(\hat{x},\hat{y})<r
, we still have
\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{y}}(\hat{A}^{\prime}_{n}(\hat{x}))>(1-\eta)^{1/2}
.

Take a finite cover of
\hat{K}
by balls of radius
r
around
\hat{x}_{1},\dots,\hat{x}_{l}
. For
n\geq N_{0}
, consider the set:

\hat{U}_{0}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{l}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{n}(\hat{x}_{i}).

Note that:

\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{0})\geq\int_{\hat{K}}\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}(\hat{U}_{0})\ d\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{x})>1-\eta.

Up to reducing
\hat{U}_{0}
, but keeping the property that
\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{0})>1-\eta
, we obtain that
\hat{\nu}_{0}(\partial\hat{U}_{0})=0
.

By construction,
\hat{U}_{0}
can be covered by a number of
\big(n,\epsilon,g\big)
-Bowen balls smaller than
\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})n+o(n)\big)
for any
n\geq N_{0}
.
∎

Step U.I. 4.

The typical
\mu_{k}
-orbits do not jump often between
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
.

The aim of this step is to show that for
n
large enough, the typical orbits of
\hat{\mu}_{k}
do not jump often between the open sets
\hat{U}_{0}
and
\hat{U}_{1}
. More precisely, we mean that these orbits have to spend long periods of time in some
\hat{U}_{i}
before intersecting the other one.

Let us choose integers
n_{1},n_{0}\geq 0
, which will be the time parameters of the orbits, and which satisfy the following:

(7.10)

n_{1}>1/\eta\quad\text{and}\quad n_{0}>\max(1/\eta,N_{0}).

Claim 7.4
.

There exist two open sets
\hat{U}_{i}^{\prime}\subset\hat{U}_{i}
, for
i=0,1
, satisfying the following.

–

\hat{\nu}_{i}(\hat{U}_{i}^{\prime})>1-\eta
and
\hat{\nu}_{i}(\partial\hat{U}_{i}^{\prime})=0
.

–

For any
0\leq j\leq n_{1}
we have:

\hat{g}^{j}\big(\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{1}^{\prime})\big)\cap\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{0}^{\prime})=\emptyset.

–

The same property holds exchanging the roles of
\hat{U}_{1}^{\prime}
and
n_{1}
with
\hat{U}_{0}^{\prime}
and
n_{0}
.

–

\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{1}^{\prime})<\eta
and
\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{U}_{0}^{\prime})<\eta
.

To keep the notations as simple as possible, we still denote
\hat{U}_{i}^{\prime}
by
\hat{U}_{i}
.

Proof.

Recall that we have the following ergodic decomposition for
i=1,2
:

\hat{\nu}_{i}=\int_{M_{f}}\hat{\nu}_{i,\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}_{i}(\hat{x}).

Since
\hat{\nu}=\alpha\hat{\nu}_{1}+(1-\alpha)\hat{\nu}_{0}
, the measures
\hat{\nu}_{0}
and
\hat{\nu}_{1}
are mutually singular in the following sense. For
\hat{\nu}_{0}\times\hat{\nu}_{1}
-almost every
(\hat{x},\hat{y})
, the measures
\hat{\nu}_{0,\hat{x}}
and
\hat{\nu}_{1,\hat{y}}
are singular. There exist then two disjoint
g
-invariant sets
\hat{X}_{0}
and
\hat{X}_{1}
such that
\hat{\nu}_{i_{1}}(\hat{X_{i_{2}}})
equals one if and only if
i_{1}=i_{2}
. Choose now a compact subset
\hat{K}_{i}\subset\hat{U}_{i}
of
\hat{X}_{i}
such that
\hat{\nu}_{i}(\hat{K}_{i})>1-\eta
. Necessarily, for all integer
j
we have:

\hat{g}^{j}(\hat{K}_{i_{1}})\cap\hat{K}_{i_{2}}\subset\hat{X}_{i_{1}}\cap\hat{X}_{i_{2}}=\emptyset

for
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. Replacing
\hat{K}_{i}
with small enough neighborhoods
\hat{U}_{i}^{\prime}\subset\hat{U}_{i}
, we can then still guarantee that for
0\leq j\leq n_{i_{1}}
we have:

\hat{g}^{j}\big(\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{i_{1}}^{\prime})\big)\cap\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{i_{2}}^{\prime})=\emptyset

for
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. Up to reducing these open sets we can assume that
\hat{\nu}_{i}(\partial\hat{U}^{\prime}_{i})=0
. Note that if
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
, we have
\hat{U}_{i_{1}}^{\prime}\cap\hat{U}_{i_{2}}^{\prime}=\emptyset
so
\hat{\nu}_{i_{1}}(\hat{U}_{i_{2}}^{\prime})\leq\hat{\nu}_{i_{1}}(M_{f}\backslash\hat{U}_{i_{1}}^{\prime})<\eta
.
∎

Step U.I. 5.

Decomposition of orbits into segments.

We call a
\hat{g}
-orbit segment with length
t
and initial point
\hat{x}
the string
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{t-1}(\hat{x}))
. Let us define the following three classes of
\hat{g}
-orbit segments
(\hat{g}^{s}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t+s-1}(\hat{x}))
of a
\hat{g}
-orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
.

–

Rectangle segments:

\hat{g}^{s}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{1}
and
t=n_{1}
.

–

Small entropy segments:

\hat{g}^{s}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{0}
and
t=n_{0}
.

–

Wild segments:
one of the following situations happens.

(a)

\hat{g}^{s+j}(\hat{x})\notin\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}
for
j=0,\dots,t-1
and
\hat{g}^{t+s}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}
.

(b)

\hat{g}^{s}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{0}
and
s+n_{0}>n-1
, in that case
t+s=n
.

(c)

\hat{g}^{s}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{1}
and
s+n_{1}>n-1
, in that case
t+s=n
.

Note that the rectangle segments and the small entropy segments are disjoint since
\hat{U}_{0}\cap\hat{U}_{1}=\emptyset
and since by Claim
7.4
if
\hat{x}\in\hat{U}_{i_{1}}
, then
\hat{g}^{j}(\hat{x})\notin\hat{U}_{i_{2}}
for
j=1,\dots,n_{i_{1}}
for
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
.

Claim 7.5
.

There exists an integer
k_{\star}
such that for any
k\geq k_{\star}
and for
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-almost every
\hat{x}
, there exists an integer
n_{k}(\hat{x})
displaying the following property. For any
n\geq n_{k}(\hat{x})
, any orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
can be decomposed as follows. There exists an integer
l
and a string:

\theta^{\prime}(\hat{x},n):=\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{l},b_{l})\big)\in(\mathbb{Z}\times\{1,2,3\})^{l+1}

such that
t_{0}=-1
,
t_{l}=n-1
,
b_{l}=1
and for any
j\in\{0,\dots,l-1\}
the orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{j}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{j+1}}(\hat{x}))
is: a rectangle segment if
b_{j}=1
, a small entropy segment if
b_{j}=2
and a wild segment if
b_{j}=3
. Moreover, we have the following estimates:

(a)

rectangle segments have total length at most
\alpha n+2\eta n
,

(b)

small entropy segments have total length at most
(1-\alpha)n+2\eta n
,

(c)

wild segments have total length at most
2\eta n
.

Proof.

By Step 1 of the proof and by Claim
7.4
, we have the following estimates:

–

\hat{\nu}(\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1})>1-\eta
,

–

\hat{\nu}(\hat{U}_{0})=\alpha\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{U}_{0})+(1-\alpha)\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{0})<\eta\alpha+(1-\alpha)<\eta+(1-\alpha)
,

–

\hat{\nu}(\hat{U}_{1})=\alpha\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{U}_{1})+(1-\alpha)\hat{\nu}_{0}(\hat{U}_{1})<\alpha+(1-\alpha)\eta<\alpha+\eta
.

Since the sequence
(\hat{\mu}_{n})
converges weak-
\star
to
\hat{\nu}
, there exists an integer
k_{\star}\geq 1
such that for any
k\geq k_{\star}
we have:

–

\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1})>1-\eta
,

–

\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{U}_{0})<(1-\alpha)+\eta
,

–

\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{U}_{1})<\alpha+\eta
.

Fix now some
k\geq k_{\star}
. For
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-almost every
\hat{x}
, there exists an integer
n_{k}(\hat{x})
such that for
n\geq n_{k}(\hat{x})
the following is true by Birkhoff theorem:

–

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathds{1}_{\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}}(\hat{g}^{i}(\hat{x}))>1-\eta
,

–

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathds{1}_{\hat{U}_{0}}(\hat{g}^{i}(\hat{x}))<(1-\alpha)+\eta
,

–

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathds{1}_{\hat{U}_{1}}(\hat{g}^{i}(\hat{x}))<\alpha+\eta
.

Fix such an
\hat{x}
. Up to increasing
n_{k}(\hat{x})
, we can suppose that
\max(n_{0},n_{1})<\eta n_{k}(\hat{x})
.

For
k\geq k_{\star}
and for
n\geq n_{k}(\hat{x})
, this allows us to define a decomposition of the
\hat{g}
-orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
. Indeed, we define the following sequence of times by induction, which depends on
\hat{x}
. Let
t_{0}=-1
. Assume that
t_{i}
is defined. We are going to define
t_{i+1}
in the following way.

–

If
\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}\in\hat{U}_{0}
and
t_{i}+n_{0}\leq n-1
, we define
t_{i+1}=t_{i}+n_{0}
. The orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i+1}}(\hat{x}))
is then a small entropy segment.

–

If
\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}\in\hat{U}_{1}
and
t_{i}+n_{1}\leq n-1
, we define
t_{i+1}=t_{i}+n_{1}
. The orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i+1}}(\hat{x}))
is then a rectangle segment.

–

If
\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}\in\hat{U}_{0}
but
t_{i}+n_{0}>n-1
, or if
\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}\in\hat{U}_{1}
but
t_{i}+n_{1}>n-1
, we define
t_{i+1}=n-1
. The orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
is then a wild segment.

–

If
\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}\in\big(\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}\big)^{c}
, then we define:

t_{i+1}=\inf\{j>t_{i},\ \hat{g}^{j+1}(\hat{x})\in\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}\},

or
t_{i+1}=n-1
if the
\inf
does not exist. The orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i+1}}(\hat{x}))
is then a wild segment.

We repeat the process until we reach some
l
such that
t_{l}=n-1
.

For
k\geq k_{\star}
, for
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-almost every
\hat{x}
and
n\geq n_{k}(\hat{x})
we can then define the following desired data:

\theta^{\prime}(\hat{x},n):=\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{l},b_{l})\big),

where
b_{i}\in\{1,2,3\}
, defining the class of the segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i}+1}(\hat{x}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i+1}}(\hat{x}))
. We introduce the convention that
b_{i}=1
means that
\vartheta_{i}
is a rectangle segment,
b_{i}=2
means that
\vartheta_{i}
is a small entropy segment and
b_{i}=3
means that
\vartheta_{i}
is a wild segment. Since
t_{l}=n-1
, we pose arbitrarily
b_{l}=1
. This gives the desired decomposition. We are now going to estimate the size of each class of segment inside this decomposition.

Fix such an
\hat{x}
and consider the
\hat{g}
-orbit segment
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
. Consider the orbit segments which do not intersect
\hat{U}_{0}
nor
\hat{U}_{1}
. Their total length is at most
\eta n
. The wild segments are segments which have this property, or last segment of an orbit, with starting point in
\hat{U}_{0}
or
\hat{U}_{1}
, which have length smaller than
\max(n_{0},n_{1})<\eta n
. This then proves the last point
(c)
.

It remains to prove cases
(a)
and
(b)
. Set
\hat{W}:=\big(\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}\big)^{c}
. Denote by
R(\hat{x})
the union of all points of the
\hat{x}
-orbit belonging to a rectangle segment of the decomposition defined earlier. Recall that Claim
7.4
implies that a rectangle segment does not intersect
\hat{U}_{0}
. We then have the following:

|R(\hat{x})|=|R(\hat{x})\cap\hat{U}_{1}|+|R(\hat{x})\cap\hat{W}|\leq|\hat{U}_{1}|+|\hat{W}|<\alpha n+\eta n+\eta n<\alpha n+2\eta n.

The argument to prove the estimate of case
(b)
is the same.
∎

For
k\geq k_{\star}
, for
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-almost every
\hat{x}
and for
n\geq n_{k}(\hat{x})
, Claim
7.5
allows us to construct the decomposition string
\theta^{\prime}(\hat{x},n)\in(\mathbb{Z}\times\{1,2,3\})^{l+1}
for some integer
l
. For a given orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
, we will also need to keep track of which iterates are inside
\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}
and which are not. For that, we introduce the word
v(\hat{x},n)\in\{0,1\}^{n}
which satisfies
v(\hat{x},n)_{i}=1
if
\hat{g}^{i}\in\hat{U}_{0}\cup\hat{U}_{1}
and
v(\hat{x},n)_{i}=0
otherwise.

We define the
decomposition type
of an orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
as the following data:

\theta(\hat{x},n):=\Big[v(\hat{x},n),\theta^{\prime}(\hat{x},n)\Big]\in\{0,1\}^{n}\times\big(\mathbb{N}\times\{1,2,3\}\big)^{l+1}.

Step U.I. 6.

A bound on the number of decomposition types.

Recall that we denote by
H
the map
H(t)=-t\log t-(1-t)\log(1-t)
. The following Claim asserts that the number of decomposition types is asymptotically small compared to the entropy of the
\mu_{k}
’s.

Claim 7.6
.

There exists an integer
n_{H}:=n_{H}(\eta)
such that the number of decomposition types of any
\hat{g}
-orbit as in Claim
7.5
with length
n>n_{H}
is at most
\exp\big(n(5\eta+2H(5\eta))\big)
.

Proof.

By Claim
7.5
, a decomposition of an orbit segment of length
n
large enough has:

–

at most
\eta n
rectangle segments (because they have length
n_{1}>1/\eta
by (
7.10
)),

–

at most
\eta n
small entropy segments by the same reason,

–

at most
2\eta n
wild segments (because their total length is at most
2\eta n
).

This gives a total of at most
\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor
segments. Any type
\theta=\Big[\big(v_{0},\dots,v_{n-1}\big),\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{l},b_{l})\big)\Big]
satisfies
l<\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor+1
. A decomposition type consists of times
t_{i}
, classes
b_{i}
and a word
v
of
n
letters with at most
\eta n
letter
0
. There can be at most
\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor}\binom{n}{i-1}3^{i}
different strings of couples of times and classes
\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{l},b_{l})\big)
. There also can be at most
\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor\eta n\rfloor}\binom{n}{i-1}
such words
v
. So the total number of decomposition types possible is bounded by
\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor}\binom{n}{i-1}3^{i}\times\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor\eta n\rfloor}\binom{n}{i-1}
. Since
4\eta<1/2
, the first sum is bounded by
4\eta n\binom{n}{\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor}\times 3^{\lfloor 4\eta n\rfloor}
and the second by
\eta n\binom{n}{\lfloor\eta n\rfloor}
. By the De Moivre-Laplace approximation, this is less than
\exp\big(4\eta n\log 3+nH(4\eta)+nH(\eta)+o(n)\big)
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
. The claim follows because
5\eta<1/2
and
4\log 3<5
.
∎

Step U.I. 7.

Conditional measures.

Let
k\geq k_{\star}
from Claim
7.5
. As explained in Section
6.2
, let
\xi_{k}
be a measurable partition subordinated to the unstable lamination of
\hat{\mu}_{k}
. Denote by
\{\hat{\mu}_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}\}_{\hat{x}}
the family of conditional measures associated to
\xi_{k}
. Recall that
\hat{\mu}_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}
is supported on
V^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})
. Denote by
\mu_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}:=\pi_{\star}\hat{\mu}_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}
. This measure is then supported on
W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})
. We fix an integer
N_{k}
and a measurable set
\hat{F}_{k}
with
\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{F}_{k})>1/2
such that every point
\hat{x}\in F_{k}
satisfies the following:

–

\hat{x}
has a well defined unstable manifold
W^{u}(\hat{x})\subset M
,

–

\mu_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}
is well defined and
x
belongs to the support of the measure
\mu_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}
restricted to
F_{k}
, where
x:=\pi(\hat{x})
and
F_{k}:=\pi(\hat{F}_{k})
,

–

\hat{x}
satisfies Claim
7.5
with
n_{k}(\hat{x})\leq N_{k}
. In particular, for each
n\geq N_{k}
the orbit
(\hat{x},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{x}))
has decomposition as in Claim
7.5
. Write
\theta(\hat{x},n)
for its decomposition type.

Step U.I. 8.

Construction of the reparametrizations.

Pick any
\hat{x}\in\hat{F}_{k}
which satisfies Proposition
6.8
for the map
g
. Recall Definition
5.1
of the local unstable set at
\hat{x}
, denoted by
V^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})\subset M_{f}
. Let
\hat{\gamma}:[0,1]\rightarrow V^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})
be a
\mathcal{C}^{r}
curve of
\mathcal{C}^{r}
size
\epsilon
, with the choice of
r>2
defined in Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
, and such that
\hat{x}\in\hat{\gamma}((0,1))
. Let
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})
be its projection. Note that
\gamma
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve. The properties of the set
\hat{F}_{k}
guarantee that
\mu_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}\big(F_{k}\cap\gamma([0,1])\big)>0
. Define
\hat{T}:=\hat{\gamma}^{-1}(\hat{F}_{k})
. Define also
T:=\gamma^{-1}(F_{k})
. Fix
n\geq\max(N_{k},n_{H})
and fix also
N_{\star},\epsilon
and
\sigma
as in Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
and Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
.

Recall the definition of the set
\hat{Y}^{\#}
before Proposition
3.11
. Recall the definition of the family of rectangles
\mathcal{R}
from Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
. Recall also that for any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, we have
\partial^{s,l}R_{i}\subset W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
where
\hat{y}_{i}^{l}\in\hat{\Lambda}\cap\hat{Y}^{\#}
for
l=1,2
. Our main goal is to show that some positive iterate of
W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x})
intersects some
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
, which will complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
. We are going to proceed by contradiction. From now on, we assume the following non-intersection hypothesis.

Hypothesis (H1)
.

For any
m\geq 0
, for any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and any
l\in\{1,2\}
we have:

f^{m}(W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x}))\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}^{l}_{i})=\emptyset.

Assuming Hypothesis
(H1)
, our goal from now on is to construct a family of reparametrization
\mathscr{R}_{n}
over
T
which is
(g,N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
n
. To simplify the notation and since we will always work with the map
g
, we just write
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible. After constructing the family of reparametrization, we will use Proposition
6.8
to get an upper bound on the entropy of
\mu_{k}
.

We begin by fixing a decomposition type
\theta=\big[\big(v_{0},\dots,v_{n-1}\big),\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{l},b_{l})\big)\big]
with
t_{0}=-1
and
t_{l}=n-1
. Recall that for any
y\in\gamma(T)
, there exists a unique
\hat{y}\in\hat{\gamma}(\hat{T})
which lifts
y
. For what follows, we will always denote this special lift by
\hat{y}
. We will first construct a family of reparametrization
\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}
which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
n
over the set:

T_{\theta}:=\gamma^{-1}\big(\{y\in\gamma(T)\ \text{such that for the unique lift}\ \hat{y}\in\hat{\gamma}(\hat{T}),\ (\hat{y},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(y))\ \text{has type}\ \theta\}\big).

We will then take the union over all possible decomposition types and obtain the desired family
\mathscr{R}_{n}
. Let us sum up what we have just explained in the following Claim. The proof will be contained in the present Step and the next one.

Claim 7.7
.

For any
k\geq k_{\star}
, for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{F}_{k}
and any
n
large enough, there exists a family of reparametrization
\mathscr{R}_{n}
of the curve
\gamma
over the set
\gamma^{-1}(F_{k})
which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
n
and such that:

\mathscr{R}_{n}=\bigcup_{\theta}\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}.

The union is taken over all decomposition types
\theta
and
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{n}
are families of reparametrization of
\gamma
over the set of parameters of
\gamma^{-1}(F_{k})
such that their image by
\gamma
has decomposition type
\theta
. The family
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{n}
is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
n
and is obtained by induction, constructing families of reparametrization for all the segments composing
\theta
. Moreover, the families
\mathscr{R}_{n}
and
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{n}
have bounded cardinality, where a bound is given in (
7.16
) and (
7.17
).

The family
\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}
will be obtained inductively by defining families of reparametrization
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}
which will be
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible over the set
T_{\theta}
up to time
t_{i}
. The first family of the induction is
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{0}:=\{id\}
. It is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible over
T_{\theta}
up to time
0
because
\gamma
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve. Assume now that
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
is well defined. We proceed by concatenation. We will set:

\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}=\{\psi\circ\phi,\ \psi\in\mathscr{R}_{t_{i-1}},\ \phi\in\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})\}

where
\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})
will be a well-chosen family of reparametrization of the curve
g^{t_{i-1}}\circ\gamma\circ\psi
and which will be
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
t_{i}-t_{i-1}
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
.

We fix then
\psi\in\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
. We let
\gamma^{\prime}:=g^{t_{i-1}}\circ\gamma\circ\psi
. Recall that
\gamma^{\prime}
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve. We let also
T^{\prime}_{\theta}:=\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
. By definition of
T_{\theta}
, for any
y\in\gamma(T_{\theta})
there exists a unique lift
\hat{y}\in\hat{\gamma}(\hat{T})
such that the orbit
(\hat{y},\dots,\hat{g}^{n-1}(\hat{y}))
has decomposition type
\theta
. We will then construct
\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})
distinguishing three cases for the different values of
b_{i-1}
. We will start with the two easy cases where
b_{i-1}
takes the value
2
or
3
, which means that the orbit segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i}}(\hat{y}))
is respectively a small entropy segment or a wild segment. The case of rectangle segment is the main difficulty of the proof.

Case A: small entropy segment.
We first treat the case
b_{i-1}=2
. For
y\in\gamma^{\prime}(T^{\prime}_{\theta})
, recall that
\hat{y}
is the unique lift of
y
such that
\hat{y}\in\hat{g}\circ\hat{\gamma}\circ\psi(\hat{T})
. Since
b_{i-1}=2
, we know that every
y\in\gamma^{\prime}(T^{\prime}_{\theta})
satisfies
\hat{y}\in\hat{U}_{0}
and
t_{i}-t_{i-1}=n_{0}
. By Claim
7.3
and since
n_{0}\geq N_{0}
, there exists a set
\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that
|\mathcal{C}_{0}|\leq\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})n_{0}\big)
and:

\gamma^{\prime}(T^{\prime}_{\theta})\subset\bigcup_{\hat{x}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}B(\pi(\hat{x}),n_{0},\epsilon)

We are now able to apply Corollary
6.7
with the regularity
r
, at the scale
\epsilon
, with times
N_{\star}
and
n_{0}
to the curve
\gamma^{\prime}
and over the set
T^{\prime}_{\theta}
. It gives us the family
\mathcal{R}(\psi,t_{i})
of reparametrization of
\gamma^{\prime}
over
T^{\prime}_{\theta}
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
t_{i}-t_{i-1}
and with the following cardinality:

\displaystyle|\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil n_{0}/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{n_{0}/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}r_{g}\big(n,\epsilon,\gamma^{\prime}(T_{\theta})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil n_{0}/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{n_{0}/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}|\mathcal{C}_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil n_{0}/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{n_{0}/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})n_{0}\big).

Doing this for every
\psi\in\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
, we obtain the desired family
\mathscr{R}_{t_{i}}^{\theta}
which then satisfies:

(7.11)

|\mathscr{R}_{t_{i}}^{\theta}|\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}\exp\big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})(t_{i}-t_{i-1})\big)|\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}|.

Case B: wild segment.
We continue with the case
b_{i-1}=3
. Since
\gamma^{\prime}
is a
(r,\epsilon)
-curve, for any
j=1,\dots,t_{i}-t_{i-1}
we have the following estimate:

\lVert d(g^{j}\circ\gamma^{\prime})\rVert\leq\epsilon\lVert dg^{j}\rVert\leq\epsilon\lVert dg^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\rVert.

Let
t_{1},t_{2}\in T^{\prime}_{\theta}
such that
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq 1/\lVert dg^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})}\rVert.
Write
x_{1}=\gamma^{\prime}(t_{1})
and
x_{2}=\gamma^{\prime}(t_{1})
. For all
j=0,\dots,t_{i}-t_{i-1}
we then have:

d\big(g^{j}(x_{1}),g^{j}(x_{2})\big)\leq\epsilon.

Construct
\mathcal{D}\subset[0,1]
a
1/\lVert dg^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\rVert
-dense set such that
\#\mathcal{D}\leq 2\lVert dg^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\rVert.
We then obtain that:

\gamma^{\prime}(T^{\prime}_{\theta})\subset\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{D}}B_{g}\big(\gamma^{\prime}(t),t_{i}-t_{i-1},\epsilon\big).

As in Case A, we apply Corollary
6.7
to obtain the family
\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})
. It satisfies the following estimate:

|\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})|\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}2\lVert dg\rVert^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}.

Again, doing this for any
\psi\in\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
, we obtain the desired family
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}
which satisfies:

(7.12)

|\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}|\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}2\lVert dg\rVert^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}|\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}|.

Case C: rectangle segment.
We now move on to the more technical and difficult case. We set
b_{i-1}=1
. Since, for any
y\in\gamma(T_{\theta})
, the segment
\vartheta^{i}(\hat{y}):=(\hat{g}^{(t_{i-1}+1)}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i}}(\hat{y}))
is a rectangle segment, we have
v_{t_{i-1}+1}=1
(here again
\hat{y}
is the unique lift of
y
belonging to
\hat{\gamma}(\hat{T})
). Moreover, since
t_{i}-t_{i-1}=n_{1}
, we know that
\hat{g}^{j}(\hat{y})\notin\hat{U}_{0}
for any
j=t_{i-1}+1,\dots,t_{i}
. From this, we conclude that if, for such a
j
, the letter
v_{j}
is equal to
1
, it means that
\hat{g}^{j}(\hat{y})\in\hat{U}_{1}
.

Set
\tau_{0}=0
,
\tau_{0}^{\prime}=0
and define the following sequence of times
\tau_{0}=\tau^{\prime}_{0}\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{1}^{\prime}\leq\dots\leq\tau_{M}\leq\tau_{M}^{\prime}
for some integer
M\geq 1
. Assume that
\tau_{j}
and
\tau_{j}^{\prime}
are well-defined. Let:

\tau_{j+1}:=\inf\{d>\tau_{k}^{\prime},\ v_{t_{i-1}+d+1}=0\}\quad\text{and}\ \quad\tau_{j+1}^{\prime}:=\inf\{d>\tau_{k+1},\ v_{t_{i-1}+d+1}=1\}

with the convention that
\inf\emptyset=n-1
. In the case where there exists
j
such that
\tau_{j}\geq n_{1}-1
but
\tau^{\prime}_{j-1}<n_{1}-1
, we let
\tau^{\prime}_{M}=\tau_{M}=n-1
. In the case where there exists
j
such that
\tau_{j}^{\prime}\geq n_{1}-1
but
\tau_{j}<n_{1}-1
, we pose
\tau_{M}=\tau_{j}<\tau_{M}^{\prime}=n-1
. Note that
\tau_{1}
is well defined since
v_{t_{i-1}+1}=1
.

To sum up, the sub-segments
\big(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j+1}}(\hat{y})\big)
satisfy that each point is contained in
\hat{U}_{1}
, while the sub-segments
\big(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}^{\prime}}(\hat{y})\big)
are the ones such that each point is outside
\hat{U}_{1}\cup\hat{U}_{0}
. Note that this last type of sub-segments are wild segments inside the rectangle segment
\big(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i}}(\hat{y})\big)
. Let us introduce the following useful quantity:

c^{0}_{i}=\#\{1\leq j\leq n_{1}-1,\ v_{t_{i-1}+j}=0\}.

This quantity represents the number of iterates in the segment
\big(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i}}(\hat{y})\big)
outside of
\hat{U}_{1}
.

We will construct the family
\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})
by induction, again by concatenations of family of reparametrizations, as explained before. We start by letting
\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{0}}=\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{0}}=\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
.

Suppose first that we dispose of a family of reparametrizations
\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{k}}
of the curve
\gamma^{\prime}
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j}
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
. We will set:

\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}}:=\{\varphi\circ\tilde{\varphi},\ \varphi\in\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j}},\ \tilde{\varphi}\in\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j}^{\prime})\}

where
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau^{\prime}_{j})
is a family of reparametrization of the curve
g^{\tau_{j}}\circ\gamma^{\prime}\circ\varphi
which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j}^{\prime}-\tau_{j}+1
over
\varphi^{-1}(\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta}))
. The resulting family
\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}}
of reparametrizations of the curve
\gamma^{\prime}
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
will then be
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1
. To construct this family, we do the same thing as in the Case B of wild segments. Indeed, the sub-segment
\big(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}^{\prime}}(\hat{y})\big)
is a wild segment inside the rectangle segment. We then obtain the following estimate:

(7.13)

|\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}}|\leq\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j}^{\prime}-\tau_{j}+1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j}^{\prime}-\tau_{j}+1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}2\lVert dg\rVert^{\tau_{j}^{\prime}-\tau_{j}+1}|\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j}}|.

Let us now move onto the last case. Suppose that we dispose of a family of reparametrizations
\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}}
of the curve
\gamma^{\prime}
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
. We will set:

\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j+1}}:=\{\varphi\circ\tilde{\varphi},\ \varphi\in\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}},\ \tilde{\varphi}\in\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})\}

where
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})
is a family of reparametrization of the curve
g^{\tau^{\prime}_{j}+1}\circ\gamma^{\prime}\circ\varphi
which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{j}-1
over
\varphi^{-1}(\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta}))
. The resulting family
\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j+1}}
of reparametrizations of the curve
\gamma^{\prime}
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
will then be
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}
.

Fix then
\varphi\in\mathscr{R}_{\tau^{\prime}_{j}}
. Let
\tilde{\gamma}:=g^{\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1}\circ\gamma^{\prime}\circ\varphi
and let
\tilde{T}_{\theta}:=\varphi^{-1}(\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta}))
. Recall that
\pi(\hat{U}_{1})\subset\cup R_{i}
, the family of rectangles obtained by applying Proposition
3.11
in Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
. Recall also that for any
y\in\gamma(T_{\theta})
, the sub-segment
(\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1}(\hat{y}),\dots,\hat{g}^{t_{i-1}+1+\tau_{j+1}}(\hat{y}))
is contained in
\hat{U}_{1}
(here
\hat{y}
is again the unique lift of
y
belonging to
\hat{\gamma}(\hat{T})
). We then obtain that
\tilde{\gamma}
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve and that
\tilde{\gamma}(\tilde{T}_{\theta})\subset\cup R_{i}
. Since
\epsilon<2\sigma(1+K)
by (
7.9
), we can apply Proposition
4.3
to the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
with the constant
C:=2(1+K)
. Recall the definition of
\text{Cross}(\tilde{\gamma})
and
\text{Fold}(\tilde{\gamma})
from Definition
4.2
, the set of crossing, respectively folding
\tilde{\gamma}
-intervals. Let
\mathcal{C}_{1}\subset\text{Cross}(\tilde{\gamma})
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}\subset\{1,\dots,L\}
be the two collections given by Proposition
4.3
.

We start by dealing with the case of crossing
\tilde{\gamma}
-intervals. Let
I=[s,t]\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
such that
I\cap\tilde{T}_{\theta}\neq\emptyset
. Denote by
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}
the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
restricted to the interval
I
. It defines then a map
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}:I=[s,t]\subset[0,1]\rightarrow M
. Let
i_{0}\in\{1,\dots,L\}
be the integer such that
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}\subset R_{i_{0}}
. Pick any
y\in\tilde{\gamma}_{I}(\tilde{T}_{\theta})
and let
\hat{y}
be its lift belonging to
\hat{U}_{1}
. Note that for any
m=0,\dots,\tau_{j+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{j}-1
, we have
\hat{g}^{m}(\hat{y})\in\hat{U}_{1}
, and then
g^{m}(y)\in\cup_{i}R_{i}
. Let then
i_{m}\in\{1,\dots,L\}
be an integer such that
g^{m}(y)\in R_{i_{m}}
(which may not be unique, since the rectangles may intersect). See Figure
9
for a visual proof of the following Claim.

Claim 7.8
.

For any
m=0,\dots,\tau_{j+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{j}-1
, the following holds:

g^{m}\big(\text{Int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})\big)\subset\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}.

Proof.

The proof goes by induction. We already have that
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}\subset R_{i_{0}}
. Suppose that
g^{m-1}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})\subset R_{i_{m-1}}
for some
0\leq j<\tau_{j+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{j}-1
. Since
\hat{g}^{m-1}(\hat{y})\in\hat{U}_{1}
and since
g(R_{i_{m-1}})\cap R_{i_{m}}\neq\emptyset
, the
us
-rectangles
\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}}
and
\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
satisfy Property
(MR5)
:

g(\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}})\cap\partial^{u}\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}=\emptyset.

It implies that the curve
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})
, which is included in
g(\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}})
cannot cross
\partial^{u}\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
. Hypothesis
(H1)
also implies that
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})
cannot cross
\partial^{s,l}\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}\subset W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i_{m}}^{l})
for
l=1,2
neither. Since
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})
meets
\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
but does not cross any of its boundaries,
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma}_{I})
has to be contained in
\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
. This concludes the induction. We emphasize that the important property that displays the curve
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}
is its connectedness.
∎

Combining Property
(MR4)
and Lemma
3.9
, for any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
we have
\text{diam}(\tilde{R}_{i})\leq\epsilon
, by (
7.9
). This implies in particular that for any
I\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
, the set
\tilde{\gamma}_{I}(\tilde{T}_{\theta})
is covered by a single one
(g,\tau_{j+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{j}-1,\epsilon)
-Bowen ball. We can now apply Corollary
6.7
to conclude that there exists a family of reparametrization
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Cross}}
of the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\text{Cross}(\tilde{\gamma})
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1
such that:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Cross}}|\leq|\mathcal{C}_{1}|\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

For the intervals
[0,s_{1}]
and
[s_{2},1]
given by Proposition
4.3
, since they are contained in some
us
-rectangle and are connected, we can deal with them as for
\tilde{\gamma}
-crossing intervals. This gives us
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Extrem}}
, a family of reparametrizations of
\tilde{\gamma}
over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\big([0,s_{1}]\cup[s_{2},1]\big)
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1
and which satisfies the estimate:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Extrem}}|\leq 2\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

We conclude Case C by dealing with the case of folding
\tilde{\gamma}
-intervals. This part of the argument contains a crucial idea, which is a bit subtle. Let us then explain it before going into the details. The important property we use in the case of crossing
\tilde{\gamma}
-intervals was their connectedness. Here, since we do not have a bound on the number of folding
\tilde{\gamma}
-intervals, we are forced to work with several intervals, but such that their images by
\tilde{\gamma}
are contained in some fixed rectangle. The set of such intervals becomes then disconnected. However the iterates by
g^{m}
of the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
for
m=0,\dots,N_{\star}
are curves which are still small compared to the size of the local stable manifolds
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
for
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and
l=1,2
. We are then able to use again Hypothesis
(H1)
combined with Property
(MR5)
, indeed, if
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma})
intersects two different rectangles of the family
\tilde{\mathcal{R}}
, since it is small,
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma})
has to intersect some
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
. For
m\geq N_{\star}
iterates, we have a bad control on the size of the curve
g^{m}(\tilde{\gamma})
and the previous argument cannot be applied. To bypass that problem, we use Yomdin Theorem after
N_{\star}
iterates to be able to work again with small curves intersecting some rectangle of
\mathcal{R}
, and repeat the previous argument.

Let
i\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
such that the set
\tilde{\gamma}(\tilde{T}_{\theta})\cap R_{i}
is non-empty. Let
q_{1},q_{2}\geq 0
be two integers such that
\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k}^{\prime}-1=q_{1}N_{\star}+q_{2}
, with
q_{2}<N_{\star}
. For
q=0,\dots,q_{1}+1
, we are going to build by induction families of reparametrizations
\mathscr{I}_{q}
(which depends on
i
) of
\tilde{\gamma}
over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}(R_{i})
, which will be
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
qN_{\star}
, with the following property. For each
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}_{q}
, the set
g^{m}\circ\tilde{\gamma}\circ\varphi^{\prime}\big((\varphi^{\prime})^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}(R_{i}))\cap\tilde{T}_{\theta}\big)
will be contained in a single one rectangle of the family
\tilde{\mathcal{R}}
for any
m=0,\dots,qN_{\star}
(the rectangle depends on the iterate
m
). Taking the union of the families
\mathscr{I}_{q_{1}+1}
for each
i\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
will give us
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Fold}}
, a family of reparametrization of the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\text{Fold}(\tilde{\gamma})
which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}-1
.

Set
\mathscr{I}_{0}=\{\text{Id}\}
. Suppose that such a family
\mathscr{I}_{q-1}
exists for some
q\in\{1,\dots,q_{1}\}
. We are going to build now the family
\mathscr{I}_{q}
. As explained previously, we proceed by concatenation. Let
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}_{q-1}
. Denote
\kappa:=g^{(q-1)N_{\star}}\circ\tilde{\gamma}\circ\varphi^{\prime}
. By the hypothesis of induction,
\kappa
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve. Let
G_{i}:=(\varphi^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}(R_{i})\big)
, the set over which we are going to reparametrize. By the induction hypothesis, there exists
i_{0}\in\{1,\dots,L\}
such that
\kappa(G_{i})\subset\tilde{R}_{i_{0}}
. Let
y\in\kappa(G_{i})
and let
\hat{y}
be its lift to
\hat{U}_{1}
. Recall that
\hat{g}^{m}(\hat{y})\in\hat{U}_{1}
for
m=0,\dots,N_{\star}
, so there exists an integer
i_{m}\in\{1,\dots,L\}
such that
g^{m}(y)\in R_{i_{m}}
for those times
m
(again,
i_{m}
may not be unique).

Claim 7.9
.

For any
m=0,\dots,N_{\star}
, the following holds:

g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})\subset\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}.

Proof.

Let
\big(\Psi_{\hat{z}_{m}},q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{m})\big)
be an adapted chart to the rectangle
R_{i_{m}}
. First, remark that the curve
g^{m}\circ\kappa
is contained in the domain
\Psi_{\hat{z}_{m}}\big(R(q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{m}))\big)
. Indeed,
g^{m}\circ\kappa
intersects
R_{i_{m}}
and for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
we have:

d\big(g^{m}\circ\kappa(t_{1}),g^{m}\circ\kappa(t_{1})\big)\leq\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}}d(\kappa(t_{1}),\kappa(t_{2}))<\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}}\epsilon.

This ensures that
g^{m}\circ\kappa([0,1])\subset\Psi_{\hat{z}_{j}}\big(R(q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{j}))\big)
, for
m=0,\dots,N_{\star}
, by the choices of
\epsilon
, and then
\sigma
, in Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
and Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
.

We proceed now by induction again. We already have that
\kappa(G_{i})\subset R_{i_{0}}\subset\tilde{R}_{i_{0}}
. Suppose that
g^{m-1}\circ\kappa(G_{i})\subset\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}}
for some
0\leq j<N_{\star}
. Note that we cannot apply directly the same argument as in Claim
7.8
since the set
g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})
is no longer connected.

The proof goes by contradiction. We know that
g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})\subset\cup_{i}R_{i}
. Suppose then that there exists a point
z\in g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})
such that
z\notin\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
. Recall that the stable boundaries
\partial^{s,l}R_{i_{j}}
are contained inside local stable manifolds
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i_{m}}^{l})
. These curves are Lipschitz graphs in the chart
\big(\Psi_{\hat{z}_{i_{m}}},q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})\big)
of size
q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})
. Denote them by
V^{s,l}
. Consider the open set
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
delimited by the Jordan curve composed by the union of the two graphs
V^{s,1}
and
V^{s,2}
and the vertical boundaries of the chart
\{\pm q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})\}\times[-q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}}),q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})]
. Since
g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})\cap R_{i_{m}}\neq\emptyset
, the image in the chart
\big(\Psi_{\hat{z}_{i_{m}}},q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})\big)
of the curve
g^{m}\circ\kappa
has to intersect
Q
. Since
\hat{g}^{m-1}(\hat{y})\in\hat{U}_{1}
and since
g(R_{i_{m-1}})\cap R_{i_{m}}\neq\emptyset
, the
us
-rectangles
\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}}
and
\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
satisfy Property
(MR5)
:

g(\tilde{R}_{i_{m-1}})\cap\partial^{u}\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}=\emptyset.

Combining this and the fact that
z\notin\tilde{R}_{i_{m}}
, we then obtain that
g^{m}\circ\kappa
intersects also
R\big(q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})\big)\backslash\overline{Q}
in the chart
\big(\Psi_{\hat{z}_{i_{m}}},q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{z}_{i_{m}})\big)
. It implies that
g^{m}\circ\kappa
has to cross one of the
V^{s,l}
, which contradicts Hypothesis
(H1)
. Indeed, we use the connectedness of
g^{m}\circ\kappa
and the fact that it has small size to bypass the fact that
g^{m}\circ\kappa(G_{i})
is not connected.
∎

Combining Claim
7.9
and the fact that
\operatorname{diam}(\tilde{R}_{i})<\epsilon
for any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, we can apply Yomdin Theorem
6.6
with the map
g^{N_{\star}}
and the curve
\kappa
. It gives us a family of reparametrization of
(r,\epsilon)
-curves over the set of
t\in[0,1]
such that
g^{N_{\star}}\circ\kappa(t)\in\tilde{R}_{i_{N_{\star}}}
with cardinality smaller than
\Upsilon\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}
. Repeating this for any
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}_{q-1}
gives us the family
\mathscr{I}_{q}
by concatenation. As explained, taking the union of the families
\mathscr{I}_{q_{1}+1}
constructed for each
i\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
gives the family of reparametrizations
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{k+1})^{\text{Fold}}
of the curve
\tilde{\gamma}
over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\text{Fold}(\tilde{\gamma})
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k}^{\prime}-1
. We then have the following bound on its cardinality:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{k+1})^{\text{Fold}}|\leq|\mathcal{C}_{2}|\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

To conclude with Case C, our goal was to construct
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{k+1})
, where
\varphi\in\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{k}^{\prime}}
, a family of reparametrization of the curve
\gamma^{\prime}:=g^{t_{i-1}}\circ\gamma\circ\psi
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j}^{\prime}+1
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
. We have built three families of reparametrizations of
\tilde{\gamma}
, all
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1
. Let us describe which set they reparameterize and what are their cardinalities.

–

\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Cross}}
reparameterizes over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\text{Cross}(\tilde{\gamma})
and has cardinality:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Cross}}|\leq|\mathcal{C}_{1}|\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

–

\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Extrem}}
reparameterizes over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\big([0,s_{1}]\cup[s_{2},1]\big)
and has cardinality:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Extrem}}|\leq 2\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

–

\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{k+1})^{\text{Fold}}
reparameterizes over the set
\tilde{T}_{\theta}\cap\text{Fold}(\tilde{\gamma})
and has cardinality:

|\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})^{\text{Fold}}|\leq|\mathcal{C}_{2}|\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

By Proposition
4.3
, we know that:

\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\Big(\tilde{\gamma}\bigcap\big(\cup_{i}R_{i}\big)\Big)\subset\text{Cross}(\tilde{\gamma})\cup\text{Fold}(\tilde{\gamma})\cup[0,s_{1}]\cup[s_{2},1].

This implies that taking the union of these three families of reparametrization gives us the desired family
\mathscr{R}(\varphi,\tau_{j+1})
. This concludes then the construction of
\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j+1}}
, which has the following bound on its cardinality:

(7.14)

|\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j+1}}|\leq\big(|\mathcal{C}_{1}|+2+|\mathcal{C}_{2}|\big)\Upsilon^{\lceil(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}^{\prime}-1)/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}|\mathscr{R}_{\tau_{j}^{\prime}}|.

By the induction process, we then have defined
\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})
, for
\psi\in\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
. We recall that it is a family of reparametrization of the curve
g^{t_{i}-1}\circ\gamma\circ\psi
over the set
\psi^{-1}(T_{\theta})
, which is
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
t_{i}-t_{i-1}
. By (
7.13
) and (
7.14
), it satisfies:

|\mathscr{R}(\psi,t_{i})|\leq 2^{c_{i}^{0}}\lVert dg\rVert^{2c_{i}^{0}}\big(|\mathcal{C}_{1}|+2+|\mathcal{C}_{2}|\big)^{c_{i}^{0}}\Upsilon^{\lceil(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{N_{\star}/r}.

Doing this for every
\psi\in\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}
, we obtain the desired family
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}
which satisfies:

(7.15)

|\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}|\leq 2^{c_{i}^{0}}\lVert dg\rVert^{2c_{i}^{0}}\big(|\mathcal{C}_{1}|+2+|\mathcal{C}_{2}|\big)^{c_{i}^{0}}\Upsilon^{\lceil(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/N_{\star}\rceil}\lVert dg\rVert^{(t_{i}-t_{i-1})/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{2N_{\star}/r}|\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i-1}}|.

This then concludes Case C and Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
.

Step U.I. 9.

A bound on the cardinality of the reparametrizations.

For each
n\geq N_{k}
and each decomposition type
\theta:=\big[\big(v_{0},\dots,v_{n-1}\big),\big((t_{0},b_{0}),\dots,(t_{m},b_{m})\big)\big]
, Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
gave us families of reparametrizations
\mathscr{R}^{\theta}_{t_{i}}
of the curve
\gamma
over
T_{\theta}
, for each time
t_{i}
. These families are
(N_{\star},\epsilon)
-admissible up to time
t_{i}
. Their cardinality depends on the class of the orbit segment
t_{i}-t_{i-1}
and the length of each class of segment is given by Claim
7.5
. Combining this with (
7.15
), (
7.11
), (
7.12
), the estimates from Proposition
4.3
(
\gamma
is an
(r,\epsilon)
-curve with
\epsilon<2\sigma(K+1)
) and the fact that
m
is bigger than the number of rectangle segments, we obtain the following bound on the cardinality of the family
\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}
:

(7.16)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})\big((1-\alpha)n+2\eta n\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\times(2\lVert dg\rVert)^{2\eta n}

\displaystyle\times\big(2\lVert dg\rVert(24(K+1)+2)\big)^{m+\sum_{i}c_{i}^{0}}

\displaystyle\times\Upsilon^{n/N_{\star}}\lVert dg\rVert^{n/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{mN_{\star}/r}.

The first term comes from the small entropy segments, the second from the wild segments, the third from the rectangle segments and the last one from Yomdin Theorem to bound the number of reparametrizations. We obtain the family
\mathscr{R}_{n}
by taking the union of the
\mathscr{R}_{n}^{\theta}
over all the decomposition types
\theta
. Note now that
\sum_{i}c_{i}^{0}
is the number of letter
0
in the word
v
, so it is smaller than
\eta n
. Note also that
m
is the total number of different segments in the decomposition type and is smaller than
4\eta n
(see the proof of Claim
7.6
). Combining this with Claim
7.6
, we obtain the following bound:

(7.17)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{R}_{n}|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(n(5\eta+2H(5\eta))\big)

\displaystyle\times\exp\Big(Nh(1+\delta^{2})\big((1-\alpha)n+2\eta n\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\times(2\lVert dg\rVert)^{2\eta n}

\displaystyle\times\big(2\lVert dg\rVert(24(K+1)+2)\big)^{5\eta n}

\displaystyle\times\Upsilon^{n/N_{\star}}\lVert dg\rVert^{n/r}\lVert dg\rVert^{4\eta nN_{\star}/r}.

Using now Proposition
6.8
, we get the following bound on the entropy of the
\mu_{k}
:

\displaystyle h(f,\mu_{k},\epsilon)=\frac{1}{N}h(g,\mu_{k},\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{Nn}\log|\mathscr{R}_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq 5\eta+2H(5\eta)

\displaystyle+h(1+\delta^{2})\big((1-\alpha)+2\eta\big)

\displaystyle+2\eta\log(2\lVert df\rVert)

\displaystyle+5\eta\log(2\lVert df\rVert)+5\eta\log\big(24(K+1)+2\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\log\Upsilon}{N_{\star}}+\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}+4\eta N_{\star}/r\log\lVert df\rVert.

Step U.I. 10.

End of the proof.

We now analyze the bound we obtained in Step 9. By (
7.7
), we have:

\frac{\log\Upsilon}{N_{\star}}<\delta/8.

Since we obviously have
\log\lVert 2df\rVert>1
we have that
5\eta<7\eta\log\lVert 2df\rVert
and then by (
7.8
) we have:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}5\eta<\delta/8,&2H(5\eta)<\delta/8,&(2+5)\eta\log\lVert 2df\rVert<\delta/8,\\
4\eta N_{\star}/r\log\lVert df\rVert<\delta/8,&\text{and}&5\eta\log\big(24(K+1)+2\big)<\delta/8.\end{array}

Finally, by (
7.3
) and (
7.8
), we have:

h(1+\delta^{2})\big((1-\alpha)+2\eta\big)=h(1-\alpha)+h\delta^{2}(1-\alpha)+h(1+\delta^{2})2\eta<h(1-\alpha)+\delta/8+\delta/8.

Gathering everything together, we obtain the following bound on the entropy of the
\mu_{k}
for
k\geq k_{\star}
:

h(f,\mu_{k},\epsilon)<\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}+h(1-\alpha)+\delta.

On the other hand, by combining (
7.6
), (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), we also have for
k\geq 0
large enough:

\displaystyle h(f,\mu_{k},\epsilon)

\displaystyle>h(f,\mu_{k})-\frac{\lambda(f)}{r}(1+\delta)

\displaystyle>\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})-\delta-\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}\frac{(1+\delta)}{(1-\delta)}

\displaystyle>(h+\kappa\delta)(1-\alpha)+2\lambda(f)/r-\delta-\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}\frac{(1+\delta)}{(1-\delta)}

\displaystyle>(h+\kappa\delta)(1-\alpha)+\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}\Big(\frac{2}{1+\delta}-\frac{(1+\delta)}{(1-\delta)}\Big)-\delta

\displaystyle>(h+\kappa\delta)(1-\alpha)+\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}\big(1-8\delta\big)-\delta

where the last inequality is a simple computation true whenever
0<\delta<1/2
. Finally, by (
7.2
), we also know that:

\kappa>\frac{(8\log\lVert df\rVert/r)-2}{1-\alpha}\iff\kappa(1-\alpha)-8\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}-2>0.

This then implies that:

(h+\kappa\delta)(1-\alpha)+\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}\big(1-8\delta\big)-\delta>\frac{\log\lVert df\rVert}{r}+h(1-\alpha)+\delta

which is impossible.

We then conclude by contradiction that Hypothesis
(H1)
does not hold true. For any
k
large enough and for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{F}_{k}
, there must then exist an
m\geq 0
,
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and
l\in\{1,2\}
such that:

f^{m}(W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x}))\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})\neq\emptyset.

Since
\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{F}_{k})>0
and the number of rectangles in the family
\mathcal{R}
is finite, there exists
i_{\star}\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and
l_{\star}\in\{1,2\}
such that
\hat{\mu}_{k}(\hat{Y}_{k})>0
where:

\hat{Y}_{k}:=\{\hat{x}\in\hat{F}_{k},\ \exists m\geq 0,\ f^{m}(W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x}))\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i_{\star}}^{l_{\star}})\neq\emptyset\}.

Recall that
V^{u}(\hat{x})\subset M_{f}
is the unstable set of
\hat{x}
from Definition
5.1
. Recall also that
\{\hat{\mu}_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}\}_{\hat{x}}
is the family of conditional measures defined in Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
. Up to passing to a set of the same
\hat{\mu}_{k}
measure as
\hat{Y}_{k}
, for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{Y}_{k}
we have that:

\hat{\mu}_{k}^{u,\hat{x}}\Big(\{\hat{y}\in V^{u}(\hat{f}^{m}(\hat{x})),\ \hat{y}\ \text{has a well-defined unstable manifold}\}\Big)>0.

We then obtain that the set:

\hat{X}_{k}:=\{\hat{x}\in M_{f},\ W^{u}(\hat{x})\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i_{\star}}^{l_{\star}})\neq\emptyset\}

has positive
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-measure.

The family of
us
-rectangles
\mathcal{R}
and the set
\hat{X}_{k}
, for
k\geq k_{\star}
are the desired objects from Proposition
7.1
. This concludes then the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

7.3.
Proof of Proposition
7.2

We move on to the proof of Proposition
7.2
. It is a Corollary of Proposition
7.1
. Before moving into the details, let us explain quickly the main points. We are going first to further decompose the measure
\hat{\nu}_{1}
into two parts, the first one,
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
, satisfies that almost every measure in its ergodic decomposition is homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
, and the second part is the rest. This gives the decomposition of the measure
\hat{\nu}
into
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
and
\hat{\nu}_{0}^{\star}:=1-\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
. The idea is then to apply Proposition
7.1
to this decomposition. We conclude that unstable manifolds of typical
\mu_{k}
-points cross some stable manifold containing the stable boundaries of the rectangles associated to
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
.

Then, since
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
is hyperbolic of saddle type with entropy large enough, we can use Theorem
6.10
and Theorem
6.11
to find transverse intersections between
\mu_{k}
-unstable manifold and the stable manifolds of a set of positive measure of some ergodic measure which is part of the ergodic decomposition of
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
. We conclude since the measure
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
has almost all its ergodic components homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
.

Step H.U.I. 1.

Obtaining topological intersections between
\mu_{k}
-unstable manifolds and the
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
-rectangles.

Recall that we have the following convergence:

\mu_{k}\rightarrow\nu

and that there exists
\alpha\in(0,1]
such that
\hat{\nu}=\alpha\hat{\nu}_{1}+(1-\alpha)\hat{\nu}_{0}
. Recall that
\hat{\nu}
-almost every ergodic component of
\hat{\nu}_{1}
is hyperbolic of saddle type with entropy larger than
\lambda(f)/r
. We denote the ergodic decomposition of
\hat{\nu}_{1}
by
\hat{\nu}_{1}=\int\hat{\nu}_{1,\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{x})
. Define the following set:

\hat{X}:=\{\hat{x}\in M_{f},\ \hat{\nu}_{1,\hat{x}}\overset{h}{\sim}\hat{\mathcal{O}}\}=\{\hat{x}\in\operatorname{supp}\hat{\nu}_{1},\ \hat{x}\overset{h}{\sim}\hat{\mathcal{O}}\}.

Let
\alpha^{\prime}:=\hat{\nu}(\hat{X})
. By the hypothesis on
\hat{\nu}_{1}
we have
\hat{\nu}_{1}(\hat{x}\in\operatorname{supp}\hat{\nu}_{1},\ \hat{x}\overset{h}{\sim}\hat{\mathcal{O}})>\beta>0
. It implies that
\hat{\nu}(\hat{x}\in\operatorname{supp}\hat{\nu}_{1},\ \hat{x}\overset{h}{\sim}\hat{\mathcal{O}})>\alpha\beta>0
and then
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha\beta
. Define the following probability measure:

\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}:=\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\hat{X}}\hat{\nu}_{1,\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}(\hat{x}).

Note that this measure satisfies that
\hat{\nu}
-almost every of its ergodic components are hyperbolic of saddle type, with entropy larger than
\lambda(f)/r
, and are homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
. Denote the ergodic decomposition of
\hat{\nu}
by
\hat{\nu}=\int\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}(\hat{x})
. Define also the probability measure:

\hat{\nu}_{0}^{\star}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\hat{X}^{c}}\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}(\hat{x}).

These measures satisfy that:

\hat{\nu}=\alpha^{\prime}\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}+(1-\alpha^{\prime})\hat{\nu}^{\star}_{0}.

Moreover, combining the fact that
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha\beta
and the hypothesis on the entropy of ergodic components of
\hat{\nu}
, we get that
\hat{\nu}
-almost every ergodic component
\hat{\nu}^{\star}_{0,\hat{x}}
of
\hat{\nu}_{0}^{\star}
satisfies the following bound:

h(f,\hat{\nu}^{\star}_{0,\hat{x}})<\frac{1}{1-\alpha\beta}\big(\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})-2\frac{\lambda(f)}{r}\big)<\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\prime}}\big(\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})-2\frac{\lambda(f)}{r}\big).

We can then apply Proposition
7.1
to this decomposition. We recall the definition of
Y^{\#}
before Proposition
3.11
. We obtain that there exists a finite family of points
\hat{y}_{1},\dots,\hat{y}_{L}\in\operatorname{supp}\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}\cap\hat{Y}^{\#}
and an integer
k_{\star}
with the following properties. For
k\geq k_{\star}
, there exists a measurable set
\hat{X}_{k}
of positive
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-measure and an integer
i(k)\in\{1,\dots,L\}
such that for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}_{k}
we have:

W^{u}(\hat{x})\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i(k)})\neq\emptyset.

Step H.U.I. 2.

Going from topological to transverse intersections.

The previous step gave us topological intersections between the
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}_{k}
and some stable manifolds of typical
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
-points. We are going to show that there exist actually transverse intersections.

Claim 7.10
.

Pick any
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}_{k}
. For any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, there exists
\hat{m}_{i}
, an ergodic hyperbolic
\hat{f}
-invariant measure of saddle type with positive entropy which satisfies the following. Suppose that
W^{u}(\hat{x})\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})\neq\emptyset
. Then for any
\epsilon^{\prime}
small enough, there exists a measurable set
\hat{A}\subset B(\hat{y}_{i},\epsilon^{\prime})
with
\hat{m}_{i}(\hat{A})>0
and such that for any
\hat{z}\in\hat{A}
we have:

f^{m}\big(W^{u}(\hat{x})\big)\pitchfork W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{z})\neq\emptyset.

for some
m\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
such that
W^{u}(\hat{x})\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})\neq\emptyset
. Let
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow W^{u}(\hat{x})
be a
\mathcal{C}^{r}
curve which contains the intersection with
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})
in its interior. Recall that
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
can be decomposed as the following:

\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}=\int\hat{\nu}^{\star}_{1,\hat{x}}\ d\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}(\hat{x})

where each
\hat{\nu}^{\star}_{1,\hat{x}}
is an ergodic hyperbolic of saddle type
\hat{f}
-invariant measure with entropy larger than
\lambda(f)/r
. Take
\hat{m}_{i}
some measure in the ergodic decomposition of
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
containing
\hat{y}_{i}
in its support. Denote by
\lambda^{u}(\hat{m}_{i})
the positive Lyapunov exponent of the ergodic measure
\hat{m}_{i}
. We have the following:

h(\hat{f},\hat{m}_{i})>\lambda(f)/r\implies h(\hat{f},\hat{m}_{i,l})/\lambda^{u}(\hat{m}_{i})>1/r

since we trivially have
\lambda(f)\geq\lambda^{u}(\hat{m}_{i})
.

Apply Theorem
6.10
with the measure
\hat{m}_{i}
and
\delta>0
small enough so that
h(\hat{f},\hat{m}_{i})/\lambda(f)-\delta>1/r
. There exists then some
\Lambda_{\hat{y}_{i}}
such that:

\dim_{H}\big(W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})\cap\Lambda_{\hat{y}_{i}}\big)>1/r.

Since
\hat{y}_{i}\in\hat{Y}^{\#}
and is in particular recurrent in the same Pesin block, and since the length of
f^{m}(W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}))
goes to zero, up to taking a big enough iterate
m\geq 0
we can suppose that:

f^{m}(W^{u}(\hat{x}))\cap W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})\in B\big(\pi(\hat{y}_{i}),\epsilon_{\star}\big).

for
\epsilon_{\star}>0
as small as we want. Since
\hat{y}_{i}\in\hat{Y}^{\#}
, it is accumulated on both sides of
W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})
by points belonging to
\Lambda_{\hat{y}_{i}}
. Up to passing to a subset of
\Lambda_{\hat{y}_{i}}
, we obtain by continuity that there exists some sub-curve of
f^{m}(\gamma)
which intersects any leaf of the lamination
\mathscr{L}^{s}:=W^{s}_{loc}\big(\Lambda_{\hat{y}_{i}}\cap B(\pi(\hat{y}_{i}),\epsilon_{\star})\cap W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})\big)
. We still denote this sub-curve of
f^{m}(\gamma)
by
\gamma
to simplify the notations. By Theorem
6.10
,
\mathscr{L}^{s}
is a continuous lamination with
\mathcal{C}^{r}
leaves and with Lipschitz holonomies. We have the following bound on its transverse dimension:

\text{\dj}(\mathscr{L}^{s})\geq\dim_{H}\big(W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{y}^{i}_{l})\cap\Lambda_{\hat{y}^{l}_{i}}\big)>1/r

where the first inequality holds since
W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
intersects transversely every leaf of
\mathscr{L}^{s}
. Recall now that
\mathscr{N}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{L}^{s})
is the lamination made of leaves of
\mathscr{L}^{s}
which intersect
\gamma
in a non-transverse way. By Theorem
6.11
, we have:

\text{\dj}\big(\mathscr{N}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{L}^{s})\big)\leq 1/r.

This implies that
\mathscr{L}^{s}
cannot be contained in
\mathscr{N}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{L}^{s})
.

There exists then some
\hat{z}\in\hat{\Lambda}_{\hat{y}_{i}}\cap B(\hat{y}_{i},\epsilon_{\star})
such that:

f^{m}\big(W^{u}(\hat{x})\big)\pitchfork W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{z})\neq\emptyset.

By continuity of the local stable manifolds on
\hat{\Lambda}_{\hat{y}_{i}}
, the same is true in a small neighborhood of
\hat{y}^{i}
. Since
\hat{z}
is in the support of
\hat{m}_{i}
restricted to some Pesin block, we obtain that there exists some set
\hat{A}
of positive
\hat{m}_{i}
-measure and
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
small enough satisfying the following. For any
\hat{z}\in\hat{A}
, we have:

f^{m}\big(W^{u}(\hat{x})\big)\cap\kern-6.99997pt|\kern 6.99997ptW^{s}_{loc}(\hat{z})\neq\emptyset

and
\hat{A}\subset B(\hat{y}_{i},\epsilon^{\prime})
. This concludes the proof of Claim
7.10
.
∎

Step H.U.I. 3.

End of the proof.

We are now able to conclude the proof of Proposition
7.2
. Since by construction,
\hat{m}_{i}
is an ergodic component of
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\star}
, it can be chosen homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
. Since
\hat{y}_{i}\in\text{supp}\ \hat{m}_{i}\cap\hat{Y}^{\#}
, we can find arbitrarily close to
\hat{y}_{i}
, points belonging to the same Pesin block as
\hat{y}_{i}
and which are homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
. Continuity of stable and unstable local manifolds on Pesin blocks, in the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
topology, implies that
\hat{y}_{i}
is homoclinically related to
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
. Using the
\lambda
-Lemma
5.4
, we conclude that there are discs of
W^{s}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
which accumulate on
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i})
. Now by Claim
7.10
, we obtain that for any
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}_{k}
we have:

f^{m}(W^{u}(\hat{x}))\pitchfork W^{s}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\neq\emptyset

for some
m\geq 0
. But the invariance of
W^{s}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
implies that this intersection also holds for
W^{u}(\hat{x})
, and then for a set of full
\hat{\mu}_{k}
-measure by ergodicity. This then concludes the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

8.
Stable with unstable intersections for saddle ergodic measures

Throughout this section,
f:M\to M
is a
\mathcal{C}^{r}
,
r>1
local diffeomorphism.

8.1.
Topological family of
us
-rectangles associated with a hyperbolic saddle measure

Let us recall some well-known facts about the disintegration of measures. Let
\hat{\nu}
be an
\hat{f}
-invariant, possibly non-ergodic, measure such that for
\hat{\nu}
-almost every
\hat{x}
we have
\lambda^{u}(\hat{x})>0>\lambda^{s}(\hat{x})
, where
\lambda^{u/s}(\hat{x})
are the two Lyapunov exponents at
\hat{x}
. The Pesin stable manifold theorem (Theorem
2.20
) asserts that
\hat{\nu}
-almost every
\hat{x}
belongs to a stable set
V^{s}(\hat{x})
(see Definition
5.1
). As in Subsection
6.2
, for a given measurable partition subordinate to the stable lamination, we have the following disintegration:

\hat{\nu}=\int\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}}^{s}\ d\hat{\nu}(\hat{x}).

We call
\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}}^{s}
the conditional measure on the stable set
V^{s}(\hat{x})
. Locally, the stable set
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
is homeomorphic to the product of a curve and a Cantor set. We can then further disintegrate every
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}
on connected components of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
as:

\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}=\int\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},W}\ d\hat{\nu}_{V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})}(W)

where
\{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},W}\}_{W}
is a family of conditionals such that each
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},W}
is supported on the connected component
W
of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
and
\hat{\nu}_{V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})}
is a probability measure on the set of connected components of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
.

The following proposition should be compared to Proposition
3.11
. Given a (possibly non-ergodic)
f
-invariant measure
\nu
, we also construct a family of
us
-rectangles with large
\nu
measure. The main difference lies in the fact that we require the unstable boundaries to be unstable local manifolds, but we do not require the dynamical Markov property
(MR5)
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
\nu
be a
f
-invariant probability measure, possibly non-ergodic. Suppose it satisfies the following for
\hat{\nu}:=\pi_{\star}^{-1}\nu
-almost every
\hat{x}
:

–

\lambda^{u}(\hat{x})>0>\lambda^{s}(\hat{x})
.

–

\hat{\nu}
-almost every ergodic component of
\hat{\nu}
has positive entropy.

–

There exists a connected component
W
of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
such that
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},W}
is non-atomic on
W
, where
\{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},W}\}_{W}
is a system of conditional measures on connected components of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
.

Let
\eta\in(0,1)
. There exists a Pesin block
\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}:=\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}(\eta)
such that the following holds for
\sigma:=\sigma(\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l})
sufficiently small.

There exists a family of
us
-rectangles
\mathcal{T}:=\{T_{1},\dots,T_{L}\}
such that:

(R1)

For any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, we have that
T_{i}
is a
us
-rectangle satisfying:

–

there exists
\hat{x}_{i}\in\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
such that
\big(\Psi_{\hat{x}_{i}},q_{\tilde{\epsilon}}(\hat{x}_{i})\big)
is an adapted chart for
T_{i}
.

–

\partial^{s,j}T_{i}\subset W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{j})
and
\partial^{u,j}T_{i}\subset W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{z}^{j}_{i})
where
\hat{y}^{j}_{i},\hat{z}^{j}_{i}\in\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}\cap\hat{Y}^{\#}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

(R2)

\hat{\nu}(\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l})>1-\eta
and
\pi\big(\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}\big)\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{L}T_{i}
.

(R3)

For any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
we have the following:

\operatorname{diam}(T_{i})<\sigma.

Before proving Proposition
8.1
, let us briefly explain the main ideas. As previously explained, this proposition is close to Proposition
3.11
. The fact that we do not require a Markov property such as Property
(MR5)
greatly simplifies the proof. In fact, we can obtain Proposition
8.1
as a corollary of Proposition
3.11
. Since the argument is very close to Step 2 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
, we will not provide all the details.

Proof of Proposition
8.1
.

Let
\eta\in(0,1)
. We begin by applying Proposition
3.11
to the measure
\nu
and with the constant
\eta/2
, with any
K>8
and with
\sigma
small enough. This gives us, in particular, a Pesin block
\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
and a family of
us
-rectangles
\mathcal{R}:=(R_{1},\dots,R_{L})
such that Properties
(MR1)
,
(MR2)
, and
(MR4)
hold.

Let
\{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}\}_{\hat{x}}
be a system of conditionals on stable local sets. Consider the set of points
\hat{X}\subset M_{f}
such that
\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}}^{s}(\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l})>0
. Note that
\hat{\nu}(\hat{X}\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l})>1-\eta
by Property
(MR2)
. Take
\hat{K}
a compact subset of
\hat{X}\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
such that
\hat{\nu}(\hat{K})>1-\eta
. For
\hat{\nu}
-almost every
\hat{x}\in\hat{K}
, there exists a connected component
W\subset V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{x})
such that
\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x},W}^{s}
is non-atomic on
W
. In particular, there exist two distinct points
\hat{x}^{+},\hat{x}^{-}\in W\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
, such that we can choose them as close as we want from
\hat{x}
. Recall that by Property
(MR1)
,
\partial^{s,l}R_{i}\subset W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}_{i}^{l})
where
\hat{y}_{i}^{l}\in\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
, for
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
and
l\in\{1,2\}
. Then, Proposition
2.16
implies that if
\pi(\hat{x})\in R_{i}
, the curve
W^{u}_{loc}(\hat{x}^{\pm})
intersects in a unique point
W^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y}^{i}_{l})
for both
l\in\{1,2\}
. The union of these four curves bounds a
us
-rectangle
T_{\hat{x},i}
such that its stable, respectively unstable, boundaries are contained in local stable, respectively unstable, manifolds of points of
\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
. Using Lemma
3.9
, taking
\hat{x}^{+}
and
\hat{x}^{-}
close enough guarantees that
\operatorname{diam}(T_{\hat{x},i})<\sigma
. Now, since the union of all
T_{\hat{x},i}
covers
\hat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\epsilon,l}
, we can extract a finite cover. This is the desired family
\mathcal{T}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

8.2.
Stable entropy

The results contained in this subsection are proved in
[
22
]
. Recall that a partition
\xi_{1}
is said to be finer than another partition
\xi_{2}
if each atom of
\xi_{1}
is contained in an atom of
\xi_{2}
. We denote this by
\xi_{2}\leq\xi_{1}
. Recall also that a partition
\xi
is measurable if there exists a sequence of countable partitions
(\alpha_{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that for any
n\geq 1
we have
\alpha_{n}\leq\alpha_{n+1}
and such that
\xi=\vee_{n\geq 1}\alpha_{n}
.

Let
\mu
be an
f
-invariant measure and let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
be two measurable partitions. Recall that
H_{\mu}(\xi_{1}|\xi_{2})
denotes the classical conditional entropy of
\xi_{1}
relative to
\xi_{2}
:

H_{\mu}(\xi_{1}|\xi_{2}):=\sum_{B\in\xi_{2}}\sum_{A\in\xi_{1}}\mu(A\cap B)\log\big(\frac{\mu(A\cap B)}{\mu(B)}\big).

Proposition 8.2

(
[
22
]
)
.

Let
\mu
be an ergodic, hyperbolic of saddle type
f
-invariant measure. Then, there exists
\xi^{s}
, a measurable partition subordinate to
W^{s}
, which satisfies:

h(f,\mu)=h(f,\mu,\xi^{s}):=H_{\mu}(\xi|f^{-1}\xi)=H_{\hat{\mu}}\big(\pi^{-1}\xi|\hat{f}^{-1}(\pi^{-1}\xi)\big).

Remark that in this case,
\pi^{-1}\xi
is a measurable partition subordinate to the stable lamination
V^{s}
. A version of Theorem
6.1
applied to
\hat{f}^{-1}
gives the following proposition.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
\hat{\mu}
be an ergodic hyperbolic of saddle type
\hat{f}
-invariant measure. Let
\{\hat{\mu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}\}_{\hat{x}}
be a system of conditional measures on stable sets. Then for
\hat{\mu}
-almost every
\hat{x}
we have:

h(\hat{f},\hat{\mu})=\inf_{\lambda>0}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{n}\log r_{\hat{f}}(n,\epsilon,\hat{\mu}^{s}_{\hat{x}},\lambda).

8.3.
Stable with unstable intersections for saddle ergodic measures

Let us now state the main proposition of the present section. It should be seen as the "stable version" of Proposition
7.1
. Recall that for a local diffeomorphism
f:M\to M
, we denote by
\deg(f)
the number of pre-images of a point
x\in M
, which is constant.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
f:M\to M
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
local diffeomorphism. Let
(\mu_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of ergodic
f
-invariant measures such that:

\mu_{k}\underset{k\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\nu

for the weak-
\star
topology. Suppose the following:

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})>\log\deg(f).

Then, there exists a finite number of hyperbolic saddle periodic orbits
\hat{\mathcal{O}_{1}},\dots,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{L}\subset M_{f}
satisfying the following properties.

(1)

For any
k
sufficiently large, there exists
j(k)\in\{1,\dots,L\}
such that for
\hat{\mu}_{k}
almost every
\hat{x}
, the following intersection holds:

W^{s}(\hat{x})\pitchfork W^{u}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{j(k)})\neq\emptyset,

where
\hat{\mu}_{k}:=\pi^{-1}_{\star}\mu_{k}
.

(2)

For any
j\in\{1,\dots,L\}
the set
\{\hat{x}\in M_{f},\ \hat{x}\overset{h}{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{j}\}
has positive
\hat{\nu}
-measure, where
\hat{\nu}:=\pi^{-1}_{\star}\nu
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
8.4
. Since the proof is close to the proof of Proposition
7.1
, but in a simpler setting, we will not provide all the details and will refer to the corresponding parts of the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

8.4.
Proof of Proposition
8.4

Recall that
f:M\to M
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
local diffeomorphism and that
(\mu_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of ergodic
f
-invariant measures. Let
\nu
be the (possibly non-ergodic)
f
-invariant measure such that:

\mu_{k}\underset{k\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\nu.

Recall also that we made the following assumption:

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})>\log d(f).

Fix then a constant
h>0
such that:

(8.1)

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}h(f,\mu_{k})-\log d(f)>h.

Step S.I. 1.

Decomposition of the measure
\nu
.

Let us write the ergodic decomposition of
\hat{\nu}
, the lift of
\nu
to
M_{f}
using
\pi
, as follows:

\hat{\nu}=\int_{M}\hat{\nu}_{x}\ d\nu(x).

Let us define the following set:

\hat{X}=\{\hat{x}\in M_{f},\ h(\hat{f},\hat{\nu}_{x})>\log d(f)\}.

Since
f
is
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
, the entropy is upper-semi continuous and we obtain that
h(f,\nu)>\log d(f)
, which implies that
\hat{\nu}(\hat{X})>0
. Recall the following Ruelle type inequality from Proposition
2.1
, which holds for any ergodic
f
-invariant measure
\mu
:

h(f,\mu)\leq\log d(f)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}(\mu)<0}\lambda_{i}(\mu)

where
\lambda_{i}(\mu)
denote the
i
-th Lyapunov exponent of
\mu
. It implies in particular that for
\hat{\nu}
-almost any
\hat{x}\in M
, the measure
\hat{\nu}_{x}
has one positive and one negative Lyapunov exponent.

We can now decompose the measure
\nu
in the following way. Let us define first the following
f
-invariant measures:

\nu_{1}:=\frac{1}{\nu(X)}\int_{X}\nu_{x}\ d\nu(x)\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{0}:=\frac{1}{\nu(X^{c})}\int_{X^{c}}\nu_{x}\ d\nu(x).

Letting
\alpha=\nu(X)
, we obtain the decomposition:

\nu=\alpha\nu_{1}+(1-\alpha)\nu_{0}.

Let
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
and let
\{\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x},\hat{y}}^{s}\}_{y}
be a system of conditional measures of the measure
\hat{\nu}_{x}
on local stable sets. Let
\{\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x},\hat{y},W}\}_{W}
be a system of conditional measures of
\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x},\hat{y}}^{s}
on the connected components
W
of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y})
. Note that by Proposition
8.3
, if there exists a set of positive
\hat{\nu}_{x}
-measure of points
\hat{y}\in M_{f}
such that
\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x},\hat{y},W}^{s}
is atomic for any connected component
W
of
V^{s}_{loc}(\hat{y})
, then we would have
h(\hat{f},\hat{\nu}_{\hat{x}})\leq\log d(f)
. In particular,
\hat{\nu}_{1}
satisfies the hypothesis of Proposition
8.1
.

Remark also that the measure
\nu_{0}
satisfies that
\nu_{0}
-almost every ergodic component has entropy less or equal to
\log d(f)
.

Step S.I. 2.

Choice of the entropy parameter.

Let
\delta>0
be an arbitrary parameter which has to be taken small enough, and which depends only on
h
. By (
6.4
), we can choose
\epsilon>0
such that for any
f
-invariant measure
m
we have:

(8.2)

h(f,m,\epsilon)>h(f,m)-\delta.

Since the way to choose
\delta
is very similar to Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
of the proof of Proposition
7.1
, we do not specify the value of
\delta
, but it will be clear in what follows that it would have been possible to make it explicit.

Step S.I. 3.

The
\nu_{1}
-rectangles.

This step can be compared with Step
(U.I.
7.2
)
in the proof of Proposition
7.1
. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be small enough, depending only on
h
and
\delta
. Again, we choose not to specify the value of
\eta
. Apply now Proposition
8.1
to the measure
\nu_{1}
with the previous choice of
\eta
and with
\sigma
small. It gives us a Pesin block
\hat{\Lambda}
and a collection of
us
-rectangles
\mathcal{T}:=\{T_{1},\dots,T_{L}\}
such that properties
(R1)
to
(R3)
hold.

Up to reducing
\sigma
and
\epsilon
, we can suppose that the following items are true.

–

For any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, there exists a homeomorphism
\varphi_{i}:(0,1)^{2}\times K\to\pi^{-1}(T_{i})
, where
K
is the Cantor set. Moreover, for any
t\in K
the map
\varphi_{i}
is a diffeomorphism on
(0,1)^{2}\times\{t\}
.

–

For any
i\in\{1,\dots,L\}
, each connected component of
\pi^{-1}(T_{i})
has diameter less than
\epsilon/3
.

Step S.I. 4.

The decomposition of typical
\mu_{k}
-orbits.

This step can be compared to Steps
(U.I.
7.2
)
,
(U.I.
7.2
)
,
(U.I.
7.2
)
, and
(U.I.
7.2
)
from Proposition
7.1
. We omit the details since the proofs are the same.

Recall that
\nu=\alpha\nu_{1}+(1-\alpha)\nu_{0}
for
\alpha>0
. Let
\hat{\nu}_{i}:=\pi^{-1}_{\star}\nu_{i}
for
i=0,1
. The following claim is obtained by combining Claim
7.3
and Claim
7.4
.

Claim 8.5
.

There exist two integers
n_{0},n_{1}\geq 1
and two open sets
\hat{U}_{0},\hat{U}_{1}\subset M_{f}
such that the following holds.

–

\hat{U}_{0}\subset\bigcup_{\hat{x}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}B_{\hat{f}^{-1}}(\hat{x},n_{0},\epsilon)
where
\mathcal{C}_{0}\subset M_{f}
such that
|\mathcal{C}_{0}|\leq\exp\big(n_{0}\log\deg(f)(1+\delta)\big)
.

–

\hat{U}_{1}\subset\pi^{-1}(\cup_{i}T_{i})
,

–

\hat{\nu}_{i}(\hat{U}_{i})>1-\eta
,
\hat{\nu}_{i}(\partial\hat{U}_{i})=0
for
i=0,1
and
\hat{\nu}_{i}(\hat{U}_{j})<\eta
for
i\neq j
.

–

For any
0\leq j\leq n_{1}
we have:

\hat{f}^{-j}\big(\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{1})\big)\cap\text{Closure}(\hat{U}_{0})=\emptyset.

The symmetric property holds exchanging the roles of
n_{1}
and
\hat{U}_{1}
with
n_{0}
and
\hat{U}_{0}
.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.1
